## Supervised tasks

| task | inputs | supervision | loss_mask | n_valid | n_allowed | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| view | deploy/obs/shard_*/<opaque>/tiles.npy (RGB views), deploy/obs/shard_*/<opaque>/view_pos.npy | self-supervised across the delivered views of one fragment (>=2 valid views anchor a contrastive row) | global_valid per view; a fragment with fewer than two valid views contributes no anchor | 590 | 589 | supervised |
| rank | deploy/tokens/shard_*.npz (<opaque>__tokens), deploy/geometry/shard_*.npz (points/normals/*_valid/scale_valid) | research/relations.parquet kind=R2 & valid -> positive pair; research/relations.parquet kind in {R1,R0} & valid & loss_mask -> legal negative | allowed = z_valid[query] & z_valid[candidate] & pair_valid; UNKNOWN never enters a loss and is never rewritten into R0 | 468 | 156 | supervised |
| correspondence | deploy/tokens/shard_*.npz (token space the weights live in) | research/labels/correspondence.parquet (index_a/index_b/weight/residual_mm/ambiguous/sample_valid) | corr_weight > 0 anchored through the token mapping, then the same `allowed` pair mask | 3299 | 0 | surface_sample_to_token_mapping_is_not_in_the_label_table |
| quality | deploy/obs (delivered observation; observation-side quality estimate) | research/labels/quality.parquet target_value (label-side observability rate per fragment x view) | valid per row; a fragment without a measurement stays invalid and is *not* a zero target | 5 | 5 | supervised |
| contradiction | deploy tokens/geometry of both sides of a pair | research/relations.parquet (kind/label_pair_valid) -> the pair compatibility class | contradiction_valid from the pair labels, then the same `allowed` mask (the head is supervised on pair logits, so both sides must encode) | 468 | 156 | supervised |

## Honest boundaries

| id | status | current value | impact | unlock condition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| no_legal_negatives | available | `{"eligible_as_negative": 440, "relation_kinds": {"R0": 1560, "UNKNOWN": 106, "R2": 28, "R1": 24, "TINY_CONTACT": 3}, "legal_negative_rows": 440}` | the rank task cannot start: a positive without a legal negative yields no gradient (effective_queries = 0) | export confirmed R1/R0 rows with valid=true and loss_mask=true between two fragments that both carry an observation |
| tokens_broadcast_defect | fixed_in_this_package | `{"fragments_measured": 59, "fragments_with_scaled_broadcast": 0, "max_sv1_over_sv0": 0.8834072050830132, "min_effective_rank": 256, "token_dim_declared": 256, "encoder_id": "identity_rgb_blockmean"}` | a token block whose columns are scaled copies of one scalar carries the information of one number per token; any layer that treats it as a 256-dim feature is reading a placeholder | re-export with the fixed writer (synlab/storage/export.py::_token_arrays, per-token sub-grid means) or use token_input |
| view_pos_all_zero | fixed_in_this_package | `{"delivered_views": 708, "arrays_all_zero": 0, "sources": ["T_WC[0,3]"], "per_fragment": [{"fragment": "LIN0009_V04_M01_PAT02_E01-m", "views": 12, "min": -86.44439697265625, "max": 59.37757873535156, "varies_within_fragm` | the declared camera-slip channel carries no information when it is all zero, so a model can not condition on the viewpoint from it; when it is real but constant inside a fragment (turntable rig: the camera stays on the slide axis) it is honest but still not a view identifier -- use frame_id for that | re-export with the fixed writer (view_pos from the delivered T_WC) or delete the channel from the observation index; a *varying* viewpoint channel needs either a slide-moving rig or a new channel in synlab/schemas/view_channels.json |
| frame_id_row_counter | fixed_in_this_package | `{"fragments_measured": 59, "index_sources": ["T_WC+K+depth_z"], "index_block": {"definition": "the delivered view whose rendered depth_z agrees with the point reprojected through K and T_WC; -1 = no delivered view explai` | frame_id == arange(n) % n_views is a function of the row index: it carries no viewpoint information and misleads any per-view pooling | re-export with the fixed writer (frame_id from K/T_WC/depth_z) or drop the array from the geometry index |
| deployment_mask_sparse | measured | `{"per_fragment_true_rate": [0.7686639361911349, 0.49489127265082467, 0.009982215033637153, 0.13464747534857857, 0.21963702307807076, 0.031027793884277344, 0.22067133585611978, 0.024664454989963107, 0.40845372941758895, 0` | the deployment mask is very sparse and uneven across views, so a view may contribute almost no pixels; per-pixel losses are dominated by the mask | raise the delivered foreground fraction (or deliver the mask contract the deployment profile really has) |
| undocumented_columns | complete | `{"columns_without_semantics": []}` | a consumer can not tell unit/meaning for those columns without the source | add the column to tools/load_package.COLUMN_SEMANTICS |
| deploy_view_channels | measured | `{"channels": ["K", "deployment_mask", "depth_z", "roi", "tiles", "uncertain_mask", "valid", "view_pos"], "beyond_core": ["K", "deployment_mask", "depth_z", "roi", "uncertain_mask"]}` | channels beyond tiles/valid/view_pos are policy-granted inputs; each one carries its own evidence_class/caveat in deploy/obs/index.json | change synlab/schemas/view_channels.json (the policy is data; the manifest records its path and hash) |

## How to load it

```powershell
& .venv\Scripts\python.exe tools\load_package.py --package "data\a12_exports\negfix\next_experiment\training_package"
& .venv\Scripts\python.exe tools\load_package.py --package "data\a12_exports\negfix\next_experiment\training_package" --check
& .venv\Scripts\python.exe examples\quickstart.py --package "data\a12_exports\negfix\next_experiment\training_package"
```

```python
from tools.load_package import load_relations, load_arrays, iter_batches
relations = load_relations(r"data\a12_exports\negfix\next_experiment\training_package")
geometry = load_arrays(r"data\a12_exports\negfix\next_experiment\training_package", kind="geometry")
for batch in iter_batches(r"data\a12_exports\negfix\next_experiment\training_package", batch_size=2):
    print(batch['index'], batch['provenance']['fragment_ids'])
```

The full machine-readable card is `dataset_card.json` next to this file.
